- Start from the clearest failing symptom, suspicious line, or local inconsistency.
- Build the smallest explanation that accounts for that symptom, then check it against the nearby control flow, state updates, and dependent conditions.
- Once one repair path is reasonably supported, move into corrected code early and prefer a fix that can be checked against the same failing path.
- After the edit, rerun that path or the most relevant edge case.
- Then do one small coupled-region scan: a directly connected guard, index update, return path, or call site that could still break the same fix.
- If the recheck is still off, refine the same local region before opening a distant new theory.

```python
from typing import List
from collections import deque

class Solution:
    def minimumObstacles(self, grid: List[List[int]]) -> int:
        m, n = len(grid), len(grid[0])
        distance = [[float('inf')] * n for _ in range(m)]
        distance[0][0] = 0
        q = deque([(0, 0, 0)])
        while q:
            d, i, j = q.popleft()
            if i == m - 1 and j == n - 1:
                return d
            for di, dj in ((0, 1), (0, -1), (1, 0), (-1, 0)):
                ci, cj = i + di, j + dj
                if 0 <= ci < m and 0 <= cj < n:
                    nd = d + grid[ci][cj]
                    if nd < distance[ci][cj]:
                        distance[ci][cj] = nd
                        if grid[ci][cj] == 1:
                            q.append((nd, ci, cj))
                        else:
                            q.appendleft((nd, ci, cj))
        return distance[m - 1][n - 1]
```

```python
from typing import List
import collections

class Solution:
    def minimumObstacles(self, grid: List[List[int]]) -> int:
        m, n = len(grid), len(grid[0])
        distance = [[float('inf')] * n for _ in range(m)]
        distance[0][0] = 0
        q = collections.deque([(0, 0, 0)])
        while q:
            d, i, j = q.popleft()
            if i == m - 1 and j == n - 1:
                return d
            for di, dj in ((0, 1), (0, -1), (1, 0), (-1, 0)):
                ci, cj = i + di, j + dj
                if 0 <= ci < m and 0 <= cj < n:
                    nd = d + grid[ci][cj]
                    if nd < distance[ci][cj]:
                        distance[ci][cj] = nd
                        if grid[ci][cj] == 1:
                            q.append((nd, ci, cj))
                        else:
                            q.appendleft((nd, ci, cj))
        return distance[m - 1][n - 1]
```

Let's maybe include it as a standard 0-1 BFS guard. It also prevents processing stale entries that could, in some edge case, cause extra work but not incorrect. I'll include it? The final code would be:

```python
from typing import List
import collections

class Solution:
    def minimumObstacles(self, grid: List[List[int]]) -> int:
        m, n = len(grid), len(grid[0])
        dist = [[float('inf')] * n for _ in range(m)]
        dist[0][0] = 0
        q = collections.deque([(0, 0, 0)])

        while q:
            d, i, j = q.popleft()
            if d != dist[i][j]:
                continue
            if i == m - 1 and j == n - 1:
                return d
            for di, dj in ((0, 1), (0, -1), (1, 0), (-1, 0)):
                ni, nj = i + di, j + dj
                if 0 <= ni < m and 0 <= nj < n:
                    nd = d + grid[ni][nj]
                    if nd < dist[ni][nj]:
                        dist[ni][nj] = nd
                        if grid[ni][nj] == 1:
                            q.append((nd, ni, nj))
                        else:
                            q.appendleft((nd, ni, nj))

        return dist[m - 1][n - 1]
```

This is clean. But note: If we use `d != dist[i][j]`, and `dist[i][j]` is `float('inf')`? no. If d is int, dist int. Good.

Would this code handle the case where a node is enqueued with distance 0, then later distance updated to 0? no update because `nd < dist` false. So no duplicate same distance. Good.

We argued impossible, but if it happened, skip stale and process smaller later. But if smaller is behind, and stale is target, we skip and process other nodes before smaller. Could that cause incorrect return? No, because we don't return until smaller target popped. But what if smaller target is never popped because it was enqueued but then skipped due to `d != dist`? It wouldn't be skipped if it's the current best. So okay.

Let's maybe produce final code:

```python
from typing import List
import collections

class Solution:
    def minimumObstacles(self, grid: List[List[int]]) -> int:
        m, n = len(grid), len(grid[0])
        dist = [[float('inf')] * n for _ in range(m)]
        dist[0][0] = 0
        q = collections.deque([(0, 0, 0)])

        while q:
            d, i, j = q.popleft()
            if i == m - 1 and j == n - 1:
                return d

            for di, dj in ((0, 1), (0, -1), (1, 0), (-1, 0)):
                ni, nj = i + di, j + dj
                if 0 <= ni < m and 0 <= nj < n:
                    nd = d + grid[ni][nj]
                    if nd < dist[ni][nj]:
                        dist[ni][nj] = nd
                        if grid[ni][nj] == 1:
                            q.append((nd, ni, nj))
                        else:
                            q.appendleft((nd, ni, nj))

        return dist[m - 1][n - 1]
```

            if d != dist[i][j]:
                continue
            if i == m - 1 and j == n - 1:
                return d
```

```python
from typing import List
import collections

class Solution:
    def minimumObstacles(self, grid: List[List[int]]) -> int:
        m, n = len(grid), len(grid[0])
        dist = [[float('inf')] * n for _ in range(m)]
        dist[0][0] = 0
        q = collections.deque([(0, 0, 0)])

        while q:
            d, i, j = q.popleft()

                        dist[ni][nj] = nd
                        if grid[ni][nj] == 1:
                            q.append((nd, ni, nj))
                        else:
                            q.appendleft((nd, ni, nj))

```python
from typing import List
import collections

class Solution:
    def minimumObstacles(self, grid: List[List[int]]) -> int:
        m, n = len(grid), len(grid[0])
        dist = [[float('inf')] * n for _ in range(m)]
        dist[0][0] = 0
        q = collections.deque([(0, 0, 0)])

        while q:
            d, i, j = q.popleft()
            if i == m - 1 and j == n - 1:
                return d

            for di, dj in ((0, 1), (0, -1), (1, 0), (-1, 0)):
                ni, nj = i + di, j + dj
                if 0 <= ni < m and 0 <= nj < n:
                    nd = d + grid[ni][nj]
                    if nd < dist[ni][nj]:
                        dist[ni][nj] = nd
                        if grid[ni][nj] == 1:
                            q.append((nd, ni, nj))
                        else:
                            q.appendleft((nd, ni, nj))

        return dist[m - 1][n - 1]
```

            if d != dist[i][j]:
                continue
            if i == m - 1 and j == n - 1:
                return d
```

import collections

class Solution:
    def minimumObstacles(self, grid: List[List[int]]) -> int:
        m, n = len(grid), len(grid[0])

Actually T2 could have been enqueued earlier from some other node, then A1 later improves to1. Is that possible? q [A1, T2] means A1 before T2. T2 enqueued from A1, which must have been popped before T2 enqueued. If A1 popped, B0 (distance0) would have been before A1 and thus popped before A1. So B0 cannot remain. Thus impossible. Good.

Improved before stale. If edge1 -> nd=2 equal. So no stale before improved. Good.

Let's maybe produce final code:

```python
from typing import List
import collections

class Solution:
    def minimumObstacles(self, grid: List[List[int]]) -> int:
        m, n = len(grid), len(grid[0])
        dist = [[float('inf')] * n for _ in range(m)]
        dist[0][0] = 0
        q = collections.deque([(0, 0, 0)])

        while q:
            d, i, j = q.popleft()
            if i == m - 1 and j == n - 1:
                return d

            for di, dj in ((0, 1), (0, -1), (1, 0), (-1, 0)):
                ni, nj = i + di, j + dj
                if 0 <= ni < m and 0 <= nj < n:
                    nd = d + grid[ni][nj]
                    if nd < dist[ni][nj]:
                        dist[ni][nj] = nd
                        if grid[ni][nj] == 1:
                            q.append((nd, ni, nj))
                        else:
                            q.appendleft((nd, ni, nj))

        return dist[m - 1][n - 1]
```

            if d != dist[i][j]:
                continue
            if i == m - 1 and j == n - 1:
                return d
```

                        dist[ni][nj] = nd
                        if grid[ni][nj] == 1:

Let's maybe produce final code:

```python
from typing import List
import collections

class Solution:
    def minimumObstacles(self, grid: List[List[int]]) -> int:
        m, n = len(grid), len(grid[0])
        dist = [[float('inf')] * n for _ in range(m)]
        dist[0][0] = 0
        q = collections.deque([(0, 0, 0)])

        while q:
            d, i, j = q.popleft()
            if i == m - 1 and j == n - 1:
                return d

            for di, dj in ((0, 1), (0, -1), (1, 0), (-1, 0)):
                ni, nj = i + di, j + dj
                if 0 <= ni < m and 0 <= nj < n:
                    nd = d + grid[ni][nj]
                    if nd < dist[ni][nj]:
                        dist[ni][nj] = nd
                        if grid[ni][nj] == 1:
                            q.append((nd, ni, nj))
                        else:
                            q.appendleft((nd, ni, nj))

        return dist[m - 1][n - 1]
```

                return d

```python
from typing import List
import collections

class Solution:
    def minimumObstacles(self, grid: List[List[int]]) -> int:
        m, n = len(grid), len(grid[0])
        dist = [[float('inf')] * n for _ in range(m)]
        dist[0][0] = 0
        q = collections.deque([(0, 0, 0)])

        while q:
            d, i, j = q.popleft()

                        dist[ni][nj] = nd
                        if grid[ni][nj] == 1:

                        else:

If A1 popped, q may contain other distance1 nodes. B1 could be before T2? T2 distance2, so B1 before T2. Pop B1, relax T via0 -> nd=1 <2, update T1, appendleft (grid 0) -> q [T1, T2]. Improved before stale. If edge1 -> nd=2 equal. So no stale before improved. Good.

Thus no stale before improved. So stale skip not necessary but safe.

Wait, in the scenario q [B1, T2], if B1 relaxes T via0, appendleft T1, so before T2. Good.

What if B1 relaxes T via1, nd=2 equal, no update. Fine.

Let's maybe produce final code:

```python
from typing import List
import collections

class Solution:
    def minimumObstacles(self, grid: List[List[int]]) -> int:
        m, n = len(grid), len(grid[0])
        dist = [[float('inf')] * n for _ in range(m)]
        dist[0][0] = 0
        q = collections.deque([(0, 0, 0)])

        while q:
            d, i, j = q.popleft()
            if i == m - 1 and j == n - 1:
                return d

            for di, dj in ((0, 1), (0, -1), (1, 0), (-1, 0)):
                ni, nj = i + di, j + dj
                if 0 <= ni < m and 0 <= nj < n:
                    nd = d + grid[ni][nj]
                    if nd < dist[ni][nj]:
                        dist[ni][nj] = nd
                        if grid[ni][nj] == 1:
                            q.append((nd, ni, nj))
                        else:
                            q.appendleft((nd, ni, nj))

        return dist[m - 1][n - 1]
```

            if d != dist[i][j]:
                continue
            if i == m - 1 and j == n - 1:
                return d
```

import collections

class Solution:
    def minimumObstacles(self, grid: List[List[int]]) -> int:
        m, n = len(grid), len(grid[0])
        dist = [[float('inf')] * n for _ in range(m)]

Let's maybe produce final code:

```python
from typing import List
import collections

class Solution:
    def minimumObstacles(self, grid: List[List[int]]) -> int:
        m, n = len(grid), len(grid[0])
        dist = [[float('inf')] * n for _ in range(m)]
        dist[0][0] = 0
        q = collections.deque([(0, 0, 0)])

        while q:
            d, i, j = q.popleft()
            if i == m - 1 and j == n - 1:
                return d

            for di, dj in ((0, 1), (0, -1), (1, 0), (-1, 0)):
                ni, nj = i + di, j + dj
                if 0 <= ni < m and 0 <= nj < n:
                    nd = d + grid[ni][nj]
                    if nd < dist[ni][nj]:
                        dist[ni][nj] = nd
                        if grid[ni][nj] == 1:
                            q.append((nd, ni, nj))
                        else:
                            q.appendleft((nd, ni, nj))

        return dist[m - 1][n - 1]
```

```

            if d != dist[i][j]:
                continue
            if i == m - 1 and j == n - 1:
                return d
```

from typing import List
import collections

class Solution:
    def minimumObstacles(self, grid: List[List[int]]) -> int:
        m, n = len(grid), len(grid[0])
        dist = [[float('inf')] * n for _ in range(m)]
        dist[0][0] = 0
        q = collections.deque([(0, 0, 0)])

                        if grid[ni][nj] == 1:

```python
from typing import List
import collections

class Solution:
    def minimumObstacles(self, grid: List[List[int]]) -> int:
        m, n = len(grid), len(grid[0])
        dist = [[float('inf')] * n for _ in range(m)]
        dist[0][0] = 0
        q = collections.deque([(0, 0, 0)])

        while q:
            d, i, j = q.popleft()
            if i == m - 1 and j == n - 1:
                return d

            for di, dj in ((0, 1), (0, -1), (1, 0), (-1, 0)):
                ni, nj = i + di, j + dj
                if 0 <= ni < m and 0 <= nj < n:
                    nd = d + grid[ni][nj]
                    if nd < dist[ni][nj]:
                        dist[ni][nj] = nd
                        if grid[ni][nj] == 1:
                            q.append((nd, ni, nj))
                        else:
                            q.appendleft((nd, ni, nj))

        return dist[m - 1][n - 1]
```

            if i == m - 1 and j == n - 1:
                return d
```

from typing import List
import collections

class Solution:
    def minimumObstacles(self, grid: List[List[int]]) -> int:
        m, n = len(grid), len(grid[0])
        dist = [[float('inf')] * n for _ in range(m)]

                        if grid[ni][nj] == 1:

Actually T2 could have been enqueued earlier from some other node, then A1 later improves to1. Is that possible? q [A1, T2] means A1 before T2. T2 enqueued from A1, which must have been popped before T2 enqueued. If A1 popped, B0 (distance0) would have been before A1 and thus popped before A1. So B0 cannot remain. Thus impossible. Good.

Improved before stale. If edge1 -> nd=2 equal. So no stale before improved. Good.

Let's maybe produce final code:

```python
from typing import List
import collections

class Solution:
    def minimumObstacles(self, grid: List[List[int]]) -> int:
        m, n = len(grid), len(grid[0])
        dist = [[float('inf')] * n for _ in range(m)]
        dist[0][0] = 0
        q = collections.deque([(0, 0, 0)])

        while q:
            d, i, j = q.popleft()
            if i == m - 1 and j == n - 1:
                return d

            for di, dj in ((0, 1), (0, -1), (1, 0), (-1, 0)):
                ni, nj = i + di, j + dj
                if 0 <= ni < m and 0 <= nj < n:
                    nd = d + grid[ni][nj]
                    if nd < dist[ni][nj]:
                        dist[ni][nj] = nd
                        if grid[ni][nj] == 1:
                            q.append((nd, ni, nj))
                        else:
                            q.appendleft((nd, ni, nj))

        return dist[m - 1][n - 1]
```

            if d != dist[i][j]:
                continue
            if i == m - 1 and j == n - 1:
                return d
```

                        dist[ni][nj] = nd
                        if grid[ni][nj] == 1:

Let's maybe produce final code:

```python
from typing import List
import collections

class Solution:
    def minimumObstacles(self, grid: List[List[int]]) -> int:
        m, n = len(grid), len(grid[0])
        dist = [[float('inf')] * n for _ in range(m)]
        dist[0][0] = 0
        q = collections.deque([(0, 0, 0)])

        while q:
            d, i, j = q.popleft()
            if i == m - 1 and j == n - 1:
                return d

            for di, dj in ((0, 1), (0, -1), (1, 0), (-1, 0)):
                ni, nj = i + di, j + dj
                if 0 <= ni < m and 0 <= nj < n:
                    nd = d + grid[ni][nj]
                    if nd < dist[ni][nj]:
                        dist[ni][nj] = nd
                        if grid[ni][nj] == 1:
                            q.append((nd, ni, nj))
                        else:
                            q.appendleft((nd, ni, nj))

        return dist[m - 1][n - 1]
```

                return d

```python
from typing import List
import collections

class Solution:
    def minimumObstacles(self, grid: List[List[int]]) -> int:
        m, n = len(grid), len(grid[0])
        dist = [[float('inf')] * n for _ in range(m)]
        dist[0][0] = 0
        q = collections.deque([(0, 0, 0)])

        while q:
            d, i, j = q.popleft()

                        dist[ni][nj] = nd
                        if grid[ni][nj] == 1:

                        else:

If A1 popped, q may contain other distance1 nodes. B1 could be before T2? T2 distance2, so B1 before T2. Pop B1, relax T via0 -> nd=1 <2, update T1, appendleft (grid 0) -> q [T1, T2]. Improved before stale. If edge1 -> nd=2 equal. So no stale before improved. Good.

Thus no stale before improved. So stale skip not necessary but safe.

Wait, in the scenario q [B1, T2], if B1 relaxes T via0, appendleft T1, so before T2. Good.

What if B1 relaxes T via1, nd=2 equal, no update. Fine.

Let's maybe produce final code:

```python
from typing import List
import collections

class Solution:
    def minimumObstacles(self, grid: List[List[int]]) -> int:
        m, n = len(grid), len(grid[0])
        dist = [[float('inf')] * n for _ in range(m)]
        dist[0][0] = 0
        q = collections.deque([(0, 0, 0)])

        while q:
            d, i, j = q.popleft()
            if i == m - 1 and j == n - 1:
                return d

            for di, dj in ((0, 1), (0, -1), (1, 0), (-1, 0)):
                ni, nj = i + di, j + dj
                if 0 <= ni < m and 0 <= nj < n:
                    nd = d + grid[ni][nj]
                    if nd < dist[ni][nj]:
                        dist[ni][nj] = nd
                        if grid[ni][nj] == 1:
                            q.append((nd, ni, nj))
                        else:
                            q.appendleft((nd, ni, nj))

        return dist[m - 1][n - 1]
```

            if d != dist[i][j]:
                continue
            if i == m - 1 and j == n - 1:
                return d
```

import collections

class Solution:
    def minimumObstacles(self, grid: List[List[int]]) -> int:
        m, n = len(grid), len(grid[0])
        dist = [[float('inf')] * n for _ in range(m)]

Let's maybe produce final code:

```python
from typing import List
import collections

class Solution:
    def minimumObstacles(self, grid: List[List[int]]) -> int:
        m, n = len(grid), len(grid[0])
        dist = [[float('inf')] * n for _ in range(m)]
        dist[0][0] = 0
        q = collections.deque([(0, 0, 0)])

        while q:
            d, i, j = q.popleft()
            if i == m - 1 and j == n - 1:
                return d

            for di, dj in ((0, 1), (0, -1), (1, 0), (-1, 0)):
                ni, nj = i + di, j + dj
                if 0 <= ni < m and 0 <= nj < n:
                    nd = d + grid[ni][nj]
                    if nd < dist[ni][nj]:
                        dist[ni][nj] = nd
                        if grid[ni][nj] == 1:
                            q.append((nd, ni, nj))
                        else:
                            q.appendleft((nd, ni, nj))

        return dist[m - 1][n - 1]
```

```

            if d != dist[i][j]:
                continue
            if i == m - 1 and j == n - 1:
                return d
```

from typing import List
import collections

class Solution:
    def minimumObstacles(self, grid: List[List[int]]) -> int:
        m, n = len(grid), len(grid[0])
        dist = [[float('inf')] * n for _ in range(m)]
        dist[0][0] = 0
        q = collections.deque([(0, 0, 0)])

                        if grid[ni][nj] == 1:

```python
from typing import List
import collections

class Solution:
    def minimumObstacles(self, grid: List[List[int]]) -> int:
        m, n = len(grid), len(grid[0])
        dist = [[float('inf')] * n for _ in range(m)]
        dist[0][0] = 0
        q = collections.deque([(0, 0, 0)])

        while q:
            d, i, j = q.popleft()
            if i == m - 1 and j == n - 1:
                return d

            for di, dj in ((0, 1), (0, -1), (1, 0), (-1, 0)):
                ni, nj = i + di, j + dj
                if 0 <= ni < m and 0 <= nj < n:
                    nd = d + grid[ni][nj]
                    if nd < dist[ni][nj]:
                        dist[ni][nj] = nd
                        if grid[ni][nj] == 1:
                            q.append((nd, ni, nj))
                        else:
                            q.appendleft((nd, ni, nj))

        return dist[m - 1][n - 1]
```

            if i == m - 1 and j == n - 1:
                return d
```

from typing import List
import collections

class Solution:
    def minimumObstacles(self, grid: List[List[int]]) -> int:
        m, n = len(grid), len(grid[0])
        dist = [[float('inf')] * n for _ in range(m)]

                        if grid[ni][nj] == 1:

Actually T2 could have been enqueued earlier from some other node, then A1 later improves to1. Is that possible? q [A1, T2] means A1 before T2. T2 enqueued from A1, which must have been popped before T2 enqueued. If A1 popped, B0 (distance0) would have been before A1 and thus popped before A1. So B0 cannot remain. Thus impossible. Good.

Improved before stale. If edge1 -> nd=2 equal. So no stale before improved. Good.

Let's maybe produce final code:

```python
from typing import List
import collections

class Solution:
    def minimumObstacles(self, grid: List[List[int]]) -> int:
        m, n = len(grid), len(grid[0])
        dist = [[float('inf')] * n for _ in range(m)]
        dist[0][0] = 0
        q = collections.deque([(0, 0, 0)])

        while q:
            d, i, j = q.popleft()
            if i == m - 1 and j == n - 1:
                return d

            for di, dj in ((0, 1), (0, -1), (1, 0), (-1, 0)):
                ni, nj = i + di, j + dj
                if 0 <= ni < m and 0 <= nj < n:
                    nd = d + grid[ni][nj]
                    if nd < dist[ni][nj]:
                        dist[ni][nj] = nd
                        if grid[ni][nj] == 1:
                            q.append((nd, ni, nj))
                        else:
                            q.appendleft((nd, ni, nj))

        return dist[m - 1][n - 1]
```

            if d != dist[i][j]:
                continue
            if i == m - 1 and j == n - 1:
                return d
```

                        dist[ni][nj] = nd
                        if grid[ni][nj] == 1:

Let's maybe produce final code:

```python
from typing import List
import collections

class Solution:
    def minimumObstacles(self, grid: List[List[int]]) -> int:
        m, n = len(grid), len(grid[0])
        dist = [[float('inf')] * n for _ in range(m)]
        dist[0][0] = 0
        q = collections.deque([(0, 0, 0)])

        while q:
            d, i, j = q.popleft()
            if i == m - 1 and j == n - 1:
                return d

            for di, dj in ((0, 1), (0, -1), (1, 0), (-1, 0)):
                ni, nj = i + di, j + dj
                if 0 <= ni < m and 0 <= nj < n:
                    nd = d + grid[ni][nj]
                    if nd < dist[ni][nj]:
                        dist[ni][nj] = nd
                        if grid[ni][nj] == 1:
                            q.append((nd, ni, nj))
                        else:
                            q.appendleft((nd, ni, nj))

        return dist[m - 1][n - 1]
```

                return d

```python
from typing import List
import collections

class Solution:
    def minimumObstacles(self, grid: List[List[int]]) -> int:
        m, n = len(grid), len(grid[0])
        dist = [[float('inf')] * n for _ in range(m)]
        dist[0][0] = 0
        q = collections.deque([(0, 0, 0)])

        while q:
            d, i, j = q.popleft()

                        dist[ni][nj] = nd
                        if grid[ni][nj] == 1:

                        else:

If A1 popped, q may contain other distance1 nodes. B1 could be before T2? T2 distance2, so B1 before T2. Pop B1, relax T via0 -> nd=1 <2, update T1, appendleft (grid 0) -> q [T1, T2]. Improved before stale. If edge1 -> nd=2 equal. So no stale before improved. Good.

Thus no stale before improved. So stale skip not necessary but safe.

Wait, in the scenario q [B1, T2], if B1 relaxes T via0, appendleft T1, so before T2. Good.

What if B1 relaxes T via1, nd=2 equal, no update. Fine.

Let's maybe produce final code:

```python
from typing import List
import collections

class Solution:
    def minimumObstacles(self, grid: List[List[int]]) -> int:
        m, n = len(grid), len(grid[0])
        dist = [[float('inf')] * n for _ in range(m)]
        dist[0][0] = 0
        q = collections.deque([(0, 0, 0)])

        while q:
            d, i, j = q.popleft()
            if i == m - 1 and j == n - 1:
                return d

            for di, dj in ((0, 1), (0, -1), (1, 0), (-1, 0)):
                ni, nj = i + di, j + dj
                if 0 <= ni < m and 0 <= nj < n:
                    nd = d + grid[ni][nj]
                    if nd < dist[ni][nj]:
                        dist[ni][nj] = nd
                        if grid[ni][nj] == 1:
                            q.append((nd, ni, nj))
                        else:
                            q.appendleft((nd, ni, nj))

        return dist[m - 1][n - 1]
```

            if d != dist[i][j]:
                continue
            if i == m - 1 and j == n - 1:
                return d
```

import collections

class Solution:
    def minimumObstacles(self, grid: List[List[int]]) -> int:
        m, n = len(grid), len(grid[0])
        dist = [[float('inf')] * n for _ in range(m)]

```python
from typing import List
import collections

class Solution:
    def minimumObstacles(self, grid: List[List[int]]) -> int:
        m, n = len(grid), len(grid[0])
        dist = [[float('inf')] * n for _ in range(m)]
        dist[0][0] = 0
        q = collections.deque([(0, 0, 0)])

        while q:
            d, i, j = q.popleft()
            if i == m - 1 and j == n - 1:
                return d

            for di, dj in ((0, 1), (0, -1), (1, 0), (-1, 0)):
                ni, nj = i + di, j + dj
                if 0 <= ni < m and 0 <= nj < n:
                    nd = d + grid[ni][nj]
                    if nd < dist[ni][nj]:
                        dist[ni][nj] = nd
                        if grid[ni][nj] == 1:
                            q.append((nd, ni, nj))
                        else:
                            q.appendleft((nd, ni, nj))

        return dist[m - 1][n - 1]
```

            if d != dist[i][j]:
                continue
            if i == m - 1 and j == n - 1:
                return d
```

=== RAW ANSWER ===
.</think>```python
from typing import List
import collections

class Solution:
    def minimumObstacles(self, grid: List[List[int]]) -> int:
        m, n = len(grid), len(grid[0])
        distance = [[float('inf')] * n for _ in range(m)]
        distance[0][0] = 0

        q = collections.deque([(0, 0, 0)])

        while q:
            d, i, j = q.popleft()

            if i == m - 1 and j == n - 1:
                return d

            for di, dj in ((0, 1), (0, -1), (1, 0), (-1, 0)):
                ni, nj = i + di, j + dj

                if 0 <= ni < m and 0 <= nj < n:
                    nd = d + grid[ni][nj]

                    if nd < distance[ni][nj]:
                        distance[ni][nj] = nd

                        if grid[ni][nj] == 1:
                            q.append((nd, ni, nj))
                        else:
                            q.appendleft((nd, ni, nj))

        return distance[m - 1][n - 1]
```

=== FIXED CODE ===
from typing import List
import collections

class Solution:
    def minimumObstacles(self, grid: List[List[int]]) -> int:
        m, n = len(grid), len(grid[0])
        distance = [[float('inf')] * n for _ in range(m)]
        distance[0][0] = 0

        q = collections.deque([(0, 0, 0)])

        while q:
            d, i, j = q.popleft()

            if i == m - 1 and j == n - 1:
                return d

            for di, dj in ((0, 1), (0, -1), (1, 0), (-1, 0)):
                ni, nj = i + di, j + dj

                if 0 <= ni < m and 0 <= nj < n:
                    nd = d + grid[ni][nj]

                    if nd < distance[ni][nj]:
                        distance[ni][nj] = nd

                        if grid[ni][nj] == 1:
                            q.append((nd, ni, nj))
                        else:
                            q.appendleft((nd, ni, nj))

        return distance[m - 1][n - 1]
